國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 服務業 PMI 由 55.4 降至 54.9，連續第 27 個月維持擴張。
商業活動及新訂單雖較前月回落 ， 仍保持穩健成長 ； 就業重返擴張區間 ，
內需與勞動市場仍具韌性 。 惟價格指數續升創高 ， 出口與進口動能轉弱 ，
後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化。 
⚫ 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月增加13.7%。
出口 3,152 億美元、進口4,208 億美元，分別月增1.4%及 4.3%。出口成
長主要受非貨幣黃金、原油及半導體帶動，進口則以原油、黃金及資本
財增加為主 。 累計前8 月貿易逆差 5,570 億美元 ， 較去年同期縮減19.9%，
惟近期進口增幅高於出口，使逆差再度擴大。 
⚫ 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示，AI 投資持續帶動經濟與就業動能，勞動市
場仍具韌性；惟去通膨進展有限，核心服務通膨及成本轉嫁壓力仍存。
全體官員支持升息 1 碼，傾向維持限制性政策，並關注 AI 融資、公債
市場波動及網路安全等金融風險。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美伊緊張情勢再升溫，川普改口不排除選前對伊朗採取軍事行動，
伊朗則持續尋求重啟談判；同時葉門青年運動頻繁襲擊沙國目標，加劇
中東安全風險。若衝突擴大，恐影響荷姆茲與曼德海峽能源運輸，推升
油氣價格並增添全球經濟下行風險。 
⚫ 美國啟動 337 調查，針對 AI 伺服器垂直供電系統（VPD）相關專利侵
權爭議展開審查，鴻海、台達電、廣達等約 20 家業者列名。由於涉及
AI 伺服器關鍵供電技術，若最終認定侵權，相關產品恐面臨輸美限制，
對供應鏈營運帶來影響。 
短評 
⚫ 美國服務業與內需仍具韌性，AI 投資亦支撐經濟動能，惟貿易逆差擴
大、成本壓力升高及通膨黏著，使聯準會仍需維持偏緊縮立場。加上美
伊與中東局勢升溫，能源價格與金融市場波動風險上升，後續景氣不確
定性仍高。

較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現分化的情形短期內仍將
持續。 
日 
本 
薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月薪資年增 3.8%。 ⚫ 9 月消費者信心下降 0.1 點至 35.4。 
短評 ⚫ 雖然通膨預期回升與物價壓力抑制部分消費信心 ， 但薪資與所得成長仍持續支撐家庭支出，使消費者信心維持溫和復甦趨勢。 
中 
國 
歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
經濟 ⚫ 中國 9 月外匯存底較 8 月減少 381 億美元至 3.4 兆美元。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國財政部宣布動用 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務額度，資金將優
先用於「六網」基礎建設等重點項目，加強穩增長措施。 
短評 
⚫ 歐中談判最終達成 16 項共識，依據歐盟發布聲明，中國將自主減少混
合動力車對歐盟的出口量 5 成以上 ， 雙方同意降低部分產品MFN 稅率 ，
預期 2027 年 3 月雙方將再度舉行貿易會談。

世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息

GDP 預估上調至 7.1%。 
⚫ 越南 2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率達 9.95%。

事件 ⚫ 印度央行升息一碼至 5.5%，以應對通膨及匯率壓力。 
短評 
⚫ 世銀認為人工智慧 （AI） 順風仍足以抵銷能源風險，但警告亞洲地區經
濟高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容易受到全球科技支出逆轉的
影響，此外，能源震撼延續到明年，補貼政策恐難以為繼。 
台 
灣 
出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月消費者物價指數年增 2.73%。 ⚫ 9 月出口額為 872.2 億美元。 
重要
事件 
⚫ 美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC)以 337 條款 ， 對特定垂直供電系統及相關
產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達、鴻海及旗下相關企業等遭
列名，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。 
短評 
⚫ 9 月出口續創佳績，反映 AI 相關需求及科技產品拉貨動能強勁，外需
表現維持熱絡；9 月 CPI 創近兩年半新高，主要受天候因素及能源價格
影響，但主計總處預期 10 月將回落。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.12 
一、 美國—美國服務業與內需維持擴張，通膨黏著與中東風險牽動政策前景 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 為 54.9，較上月
55.4 下降 0.5 個百分點，已連續第 27 個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近 12
個月平均值 54.1，服務業景氣仍持續穩健擴張。細項觀察，商業活動指數由 61.7
降至 56.5，新訂單指數由 60.9 降至 59.8，雖較前月回落，但仍維持高檔擴張；就
業指數則由 47.8 回升至 50.1，三個月來首度重返擴張區間。價格指數由 72.6 升
至 74.0，創 2022 年 7 月以來新高。供應商交貨指數由 51.3 升至 53.2，交貨時間
再度延長；庫存指數由56.7 升至 57.8，企業持續提前備貨以因應未來成本上升風
險。此外，未完成訂單指數升至 56.6，創 2022 年 7 月以來高點，惟新出口訂單
指數由 56.3 大幅降至 46.9，八個月來首度跌入收縮區間，進口指數亦由 56.3 降
至 52.9，外部需求及採購動能均降溫。整體而言，美國服務業景氣仍維持穩健擴
張，內需與就業展現一定韌性，但燃料價格上漲、關稅政策、供應鏈限制及地緣
政治等因素持續推升企業成本，後續仍須關注通膨壓力及外需變化對景氣的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 1,056 億美元 ， 較上月修正後928
億美元增加 127 億美元，增幅 13.7%。當月出口 3,152 億美元，較上月增加 45 億
美元(+1.4%)；進口 4,208 億美元，較上月增加 172 億美元(+4.3%)。其中，商品貿
易逆差擴大至 1,366 億美元，服務貿易順差則小幅增至 310 億美元。出口成長主
要受工業用品及原材料出口增加 63 億美元帶動，其中非貨幣黃金、原油及燃油
出口增幅較為顯著，資本財出口亦受半導體、電腦及電腦周邊設備需求增加所支
撐；進口方面，工業用品及原材料進口增加 91 億美元，資本財進口增加 62 億美
元，其中原油、非貨幣黃金、半導體及工業機械進口增幅較大。累計2026 年 1 至
8 月，美國商品及服務貿易逆差為 5,570 億美元，較 2025 年同期減少 1,382 億美
元，縮減 19.9%；同期出口成長 11.8%，進口增加 4.4%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡
情況較去年同期有所改善，惟近期進口增幅明顯高於出口，使貿易逆差再度呈現
擴大趨勢。 
2026 年 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要重點如下：(1)經濟：若干官員認為美國經濟成
長動能有所增強 ，AI 投資規模與速度持續超乎預期 ， 帶動企業投資與生產力提升 ；

企業活動則受到企業獲利維持高檔及部分政策因素支持；(2)就業：多數官員表示
勞動市場近期略有改善，就業成長維持穩健，失業率仍處低檔，整體勞動市場大
致符合充分就業狀態，供需風險大致平衡；(3)通膨：官員普遍認為近期去通膨進
展有限，非住房核心服務通膨仍偏高，人力、能源及部分進口成本轉嫁情形仍值
得關注；部分官員擔憂 AI 投資與相關支出擴張，可能推升需求並增加通膨持續
性風險；(4)貨幣政策：鑑於經濟活動持續擴張、勞動市場具韌性且通膨仍高於目
標，全體官員支持升息 1 碼。許多官員認為維持較具限制性的政策立場有助降低
通膨風險，部分官員則認為未來可能仍需進一步緊縮政策，以確保通膨回到目標
水準；(5)金融風險：官員普遍認為金融條件整體仍支持經濟成長，但部分官員關
切 AI 基礎設施快速擴張所衍生的融資風險，以及美國公債市場波動、網路安全
與其他潛在金融穩定風險。 
近期美伊及中東局勢再度升溫，市場對區域衝突擴大的疑慮升高。美國總統
川普由先前宣稱期中選舉前不會攻擊伊朗 ， 改口表示 「仍有可能」 採取軍事行動 ，
且美軍已就相關軍事選項進行準備，使美伊對抗風險再度攀升；另一方面，伊朗
雖有意透過斡旋重啟談判，但雙方在核計畫與制裁議題上仍存重大分歧。此外，
葉門青年運動持續升高對沙烏地阿拉伯攻擊，利雅德哈立德國王國際機場一週內
多次遇襲，造成多人死傷，凸顯區域安全風險升高。整體而言，若美伊衝突或代
理人戰事進一步升級，恐衝擊荷姆茲海峽及曼德海峽等能源運輸要道，推升國際
油氣價格、加劇通膨壓力，並增添全球金融市場波動及經濟成長下行風險。 
美國國際貿易委員會(USITC)於 10 月 9 日正式啟動 337 條款調查，針對特定
垂直供電系統(Vertical Power Delivery，VPD)、 相關零組件及採用該技術的運算設
備是否涉及專利侵權展開審查。此案由美國電源技術廠商 Vicor Corporation 提出
申訴，主張相關產品侵犯其專利權，並要求發布有限排除令及停止侵權命令，以
限制涉案產品進入美國市場 。 調查對象涵蓋全球約20 家企業 ， 包括台灣的鴻海 、
台達電、廣達及其美國關係企業，另有中國的立訊精密、工業富聯、德國的英飛
凌等國際大廠列名 。 本案聚焦 AI 伺服器及高效能運算領域關鍵的供電架構技術 ，
該技術有助提升晶片供電效率、降低能源損耗，已廣泛應用於資料中心與 AI 運
算設備。隨著全球 AI 基礎建設投資持續擴大，相關專利布局與授權爭議亦日益
升溫。USITC 指出，此次立案僅代表調查程序正式展開，並不表示已認定侵權，
後續將由行政法官進行聽證及審理，預計於 45 天內訂定調查時程。台灣經濟部

則表示，337 調查屬企業間智慧財產權爭議，政府將持續關注案情發展，並於必
要時提供業者相關協助 ； 若最終認定違反337 條款 ， 涉案產品可能面臨輸美限制 ，
對 AI 伺服器供應鏈帶來潛在影響。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率短期內恐難降溫 
 德國工業生產在 7 月月減 1.2%後，8 月回彈 2%，主要反映部分汽車業者在 7
月提前進行歲修 ， 並且在8 月陸續復工後 ， 帶動生產及庫存回補需求所致 ； 此外 ，
受到 8 月萊茵河水位降低，沿岸化學工廠物流運輸受阻，致化工產業產出月減
1.2%為主要拖累因素。儘管工業生產如期反彈，惟工業訂單卻大幅下跌 10.6%，
主要是與軍工產業相關的訂單如飛機、船舶、軍用車輛、武器彈藥等訂單減少，
顯示政府前期大額採購效應逐漸消退；除軍工產業外，其餘產業訂單則大致維持
穩定。 
 法國 8 月工業生產月減 0.3%，為連續第二個月下滑，主要係夏季用電高峰過
後電力產出回落，帶動能源部門生產減少所致。另方面，製造業生產月增0.3%，
其中造紙、化工及電子電機設備等產業自前期低點回升，部分抵銷汽車等運輸設
備產出月減 2.2%的拖累。不過，法國 9 月 INSEE 新訂單指標由 8 月的-13.8 點降
至-15.8 點，顯示企業接單偏弱，主要反映出口需求疲軟。此外，相較於德國近期
透過國防及基礎建設支出擴大支撐工業生產，法國財政擴張力道相對有限，使製
造業景氣較依賴外部需求，德、法兩國製造業表現因而呈現逐漸分化的趨勢。 
 展望未來，近期歐元區各國殖利率上升，除反映通膨壓力增強、升息預增加
之外，亦部分反映西班牙宣布於 11 月 29 日提前進行大選、法國財政前景不明朗
等政治因素影響。儘管較高的殖利率有助於促使法國政府推動財政整頓，但考量
相關改革涉及福利及退休制度調整等政治敏感議題，不確定性較高，短期內德國
與法國殖利率差距恐難縮小。整體而言，法國製造業在出口需求疲弱及財政支撐
相對有限下，景氣復甦力道恐仍較為溫和，預料德、法兩國經濟及工業活動表現
分化的情形短期內仍將持續。 
三、日本—薪資成長支撐內需，消費信心持穩 
 8 月薪資年增 3.8%，其中經常性薪資年增 3.8%、加班費年增 5.2%、實質薪資
年增 1.5%， 主要受惠於春鬥加薪效應延續及通膨壓力較去年趨緩所帶動 ， 有助於
進一步提升民眾購買力，支撐消費與經濟復甦。

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

9 月消費者信心指數小幅下滑 0.1 點至 35.4，但表現仍優於市場預期。受食品
及商品價格持續上漲影響 ， 民眾對未來一年通膨的預期升溫 ， 預期通膨率高於2%
的家庭比例升至 79.3%，並使生活水準及耐久財購買意願略為轉弱。不過，穩健
的薪資成長及股市上漲帶來的金融收入，持續支撐家庭所得，有助於緩衝物價上
漲對消費的負面影響，顯示日本內需仍具一定韌性。 
四、中國—歐中達成貿易談判，外部風險暫時降溫 
中國 9 月外匯存底降至 3.40 兆美元，較前月減少 381 億美元，反映美元升
值、政府以外匯購買黃金儲備，加上歐美殖利率上升，造成中國持有的海外債券
資產出現評價損失，皆為外匯存底下滑的主要原因。惟亦不排除中國外匯流入情
況可能有所轉弱，例如出口成長放緩、進口需求增加，或資本外流規模高於預期
等因素。 
展望未來 ， 近期歐中談判達成16 項共識 ， 未來中國混合動力車出口至歐盟將
面臨調整壓力，但歐盟進一步升高貿易保護措施的風險已降溫。另一方面，基於
中國維持大額經常帳順差、出口商結匯需求及年底季節性資金流入支撐，預期人
民幣仍有升值空間，並帶動外匯存底回升。 
五、印度及東南亞—世銀上調經濟展望；印度央行近四年來首度升息 
(1)世界銀行 2026 年 10 月《東亞與太平洋地區經濟展望報告》指出，受人工智慧
（AI） 相關出口與投資強勁帶動，將2026 年東亞與太平洋地區 （EAP） 經濟成長
率預測上修 0.3 個百分點至 4.5%，2027 年與 2028 年將分別放緩至 4.4%與 4.3%，
其中，越南、馬來西亞、泰國 GDP 大幅上調至 7.4%、5.1%及 2.0%。世銀指出，
AI 相關商品占多數經濟體出口增長的一半以上，其中，馬來西亞、菲律賓、泰國
和越南的 AI 出口貢獻率更超過七成，但扣除 AI 相關產品後，區域貿易成長呈現
疲弱或負增長，警告該地區的經濟韌性高度依賴 AI 相關製造業和出口，使其容
易受到全球科技支出逆轉的影響，建議應將 AI 產品生產優勢轉化為廣泛應用，
以提升生產力，並創造更多優質就業機會。此外，美伊戰爭推升能源價格，各國
政府比其他地區更積極因應 ， 也更仰賴補貼 ， 此將增加財政成本並減少外匯存底 ，
世銀預估中東石油出口要到 2027 年中才會恢復至戰前水準，能源成本上升已對
製造業造成壓力 ， 運輸價格大漲也已打擊消費需求 ， 持續性的補貼可能難以為繼 。

用相關內容。

8

(2)印度:印度央行 10/7 貨幣政策會議宣布升息 1 碼至 5.5%，為近四年來首度升
息，貨幣政策立場從「中性」調整為「有節制的緊縮」。央行總裁指出，目前已
有跡象顯示，通膨預期升高，且價格壓力正逐漸擴散至更廣泛的領域。經濟展望
方面，印度內需、投資及基礎建設支出持續支撐景氣，央行將 2026/27 財年經濟
成長率預測由 6.7%上調至 7.1%，另受中東局勢緊張、國際油價上漲、食品價格
攀升及氣候因素影響，將 2026/27 財年 CPI 通膨率預測上調至 5.2%，高於 4%的
中期目標。另外，世界銀行 10 月最新《南亞經濟展望》報告，上修印度 2026/27
財年經濟成長率 0.4 個百分點至 7.1%，反映內需消費、製造業、服務業及出口表
現優於預期，然而，中東衝突導致能源價格上漲、聖嬰現象衝擊農業生產、全球
金融市場波動及資本流動風險，仍可能影響經濟前景。整體而言，印度經濟成長
動能仍穩健，但外部風險及輸入性通膨壓力增加，未來貨幣政策將以維持物價穩
定為優先，並有進一步升息的可能。 
 (3)越南: 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率達 9.95%，高於上季修正後的 8.81%，並
創 4 年新高，累計前三季經濟成長率達 9.01%。成長動能主要來自製造業與營建
業擴張、外人直接投資持續流入、以及外貿大幅成長。各產業均穩健成長，工業
及建築業成長 12.50%(占 GDP 總值 51.57%)；服務業成長 9.54%(占 43.29%)；農
業成長 4.21%(占 5.14%)。以支出面觀之，民間消費成長 8.96%，固定投資成長
21.39%，貨物和服務出、進口分別成長23.27%、28.75%。展望未來，越南受惠於
全球企業分散供應鏈、AI 產業需求及政府推動基礎建設與數位轉型 ， 經濟前景仍
偏正向。

六、台灣—出口動能強勁，物價壓力與貿易風險並存 
9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.73%，創近兩年半新高，已連續 5 個
月高於 2%警戒線，主要係受豪雨影響，蔬果供應減少帶動價格上漲，加上汽機
車減稅效應淡化、中秋節落點不同及國際油價維持高檔，共同推升物價。除蔬果
外，外食費、房租、電費及交通相關費用皆持續上漲，顯示物價壓力仍在，但主
計總處認為整體漲勢仍屬溫和，預估 10 月 CPI 將略為回落至約 2.5%。 
9 月出口額達 872.2 億美元，年增 60.9%，其中資通產品出口倍增、電子零組
件年增 45.3%。財政部指出，在AI 相關投資持續、部分電子產品供應偏緊、新品
上市與旺季拉貨等因素共同挹注下，出口表現明顯升溫；隨著 AI 應用擴大及年
底採購需求增加，全年出口額有望突破 9,000 億美元。 
美國國際貿易委員會 （USITC）於10 月 9 日依據 《1930 年關稅法》 第337 條
款，對特定垂直供電系統及相關產品啟動專利侵權調查，其中台達電、廣達及鴻
海旗下相關企業等遭列名。若 337 條款調查最終認定侵權，相關產品可能遭禁止
輸入美國，後續應關注調查結果及對台灣電源供應與伺服器供應鏈的影響。

### Page 6

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Chroma ATE
Company description
Chroma ATE is a leading supplier of testing and instrumentation equipment,
automated testing systems (ATS), and turnkey solutions. Its products are
used for testing power electronics, semiconductor, IC, EV component,
display, and other electronics applications.
Investment strategy
We rate Chroma Buy as we expect structural and robust growth ahead, driven
by: 1) strong power tester demand for AI data center/servers; 2) expanding SLT

(system level testing) demand driven by new generation model launch and
new customers ramp-up; 3) strong metrology growth from increasing foundry
capex investment in advanced packaging; and 4) solid silicon photonics
equipment demand, with CPO a key incremental upside.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG.
Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical range as its growth outlook
is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its

revenue are now AI-related, with strong growth across product segments
including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to
capitalized on the global AI expansion.
Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth
expectation due to changes in capex outlook or production bottleneck; 2)
fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Hon Precision (7769.TW): Large package and CPO on track to drive 2027 
content growth; 3Q26 beat; TP up to NT$12,600; Buy
29 October 2026 | 3:16AM HKT
Hon Precision delivered better-than-expected 3Q26 results, and highlighted: 1) 
product mix upgrade: a higher ASP for its large-package (Mega Tray) solution, with 
20-30% of some customers’ new orders already on Mega Tray, 2) CPO Insertion 4 
customer qualification expected to finish by 2026 year-end, with mass production 
targeted in 1Q27, 3) a reiterated capacity expansion target of +50% for 2027, and 4) 
new projects expected to lift GM from 2H27. We remain positive on Hon Precision 
and raise our 12m TP to NT$12,600 (implied 49x 2027E P/E vs. 83% NI YoY in 
2027-28E on avg.). Maintain Buy. 
ATC platforms across FT and SLT to meet customers’ needs 
During the meeting, management laid out its ATC roadmap in detail, covering three 
platforms. ATC 3 uses liquid cooling and is adopted by an ASIC customer, with fast 
temperature ramp, c.10kW of power (vs. market mainstream of 2-3kW) and a 
25-125°C range. ATC 5 uses refrigerant and supports tri-temp (-70-125°C) at 
c.10kW, mainly for automotive customers. ATC 8 uses refrigerant with c.25kW of 
power, for high-power applications. For large packages such as 200mm x 200mm, 
management plans to handle 12kW by end-2026 and 25kW by end-2027. Overall, 
Hon offers a different ATC system for each customer’s test requirements and installs 
it on its FT or SLT handlers. Management believes this ability to meet varying 
temperature and power needs is why customers favor Hon’s products. 
Mega Tray solution across FT and SLT supports a higher ASP uplift 
The large-package (Mega Tray) solution targets end-customer packages larger than 
120mm x 150mm and is backward-compatible with current chip sizes. Small-volume 
shipments start in 2H26 and penetration is anticipated to rise in 2027. In final test 
(FT), Hon offers the HP9050, a dual-temp model, and the HP1050, a tri-temp model, 
which can be paired with different ATC platforms depending on customer needs. In 
system-level test (SLT), Hon offers the HP-3036, which supports 36 test boards in 
parallel (vs. 6-12 today), with an ASP more than 2x current, as longer test times make 
parallel testing increasingly important. Management emphasized the importance of 
integrating full automation and advanced AOI into these new handlers. The handlers 
can also be paired with Autonomous Mobile Robots (AMRs), which OSAT customers 
are actively adopting. On ASP , Mega Tray enjoys better price vs. the mainstream 
handler solution. For some customers, 20-30% of new orders are already on Mega 
Tray.

### Page 2

CPO qualification by 2026 year-end 
Management said its CPO Insertion 4 tool has been ready for mass production since 
end-October, with current shipments mainly small-volume engineering tools. Customer 
qualification starts in November and is expected to finish in December. The customer 
targets mass production in 1Q27, and Hon has prepared long lead time materials in 
advance. 
The tool tests simultaneously for optical and electrical performance. This requires two 
thermal systems, one for the ASIC and a separate system for the OEs, while ATE handles 
the electrical test. Hon’s in-house optical alignment stage is still under qualification. 
For CPC, Hon is focused on mechanical alignment. Some customers have strong demand 
and are looking to complete NPI as soon as possible. Hon is co-developing with a US 
networking customer and a major ATE vendor. 
2027 capacity expansion reiterated 
Looking into 2027, management reiterated its capacity expansion target of +50%. With 
order visibility for the next 2-3 quarters, we expect revenue to keep growing into 1H27. 
On margins, high-end ATC and Mega Tray shipments start in 4Q26, and CPO tools begin 
delivery in 1Q27. With a 3-6 month lag for equipment revenue recognition, management 
expects these new products to drive GM higher in 2H27. 
On competition, management said major OSATs currently mainly use Hon’s equipment, 
and challengers are mostly at an early stage. Management sees its advantage in ATC 
technology, where it leads peers, as well as a more complete product portfolio given 
widely varying customer requirements. On CPO, management sees its optical and 
electrical co-testing tool as next-generation equipment, leading in the market. 
Earnings revision and Valuation

3Q26 beat: Hon Precision’s 3Q26 revenue of NT$16.2bn (+17.4% QoQ, +97.5% YoY) 
came in 5.0% above GSe and 7.4% above Bloomberg consensus. Margin-wise, 3Q26 GM 
of 56.9% (vs. 56.1% in 2Q26) was in line with GSe/cons of 56.5%/57.0%. For opex, the 
company’s 3Q26 opex ratio of 7.3% was higher vs. GSe/cons of 5.6%/6.6%, primarily 
due to higher R&D expense. As a result, 3Q26 EPS of NT$37.33 came in 4.7% above GSe 
of NT$35.64 and 8.0% above cons of NT$34.61.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and raise our EPS by 2% / 2% / 1% in 
2026-28E mainly on higher revenues and GM, reflecting the better-than-expected 3Q26

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Hon Precision (7769.TW) 
Valuation: We are Buy rated on Hon Precision. Our 12-month TP of NT$12,600 is based 
on a target P/E of 32x (based on close peer correlation analysis) applied to our 2028E 
EPS, and discounted back to 2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: 
(1) 1.5x beta, (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) softer AI/HPC demand, (2) slower adoption of SLT 
among AI ASICs, and (3) intensifying competition.

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide an update on AVC. The company plans to release 
its 3Q26 results on 10 November. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call (see our 
latest memo Record-high 3Q26 revenue beat expectations, 7 October). We 
expect AVC to maintain its leading position in the following years with more 
content value from ASICs projects since 4Q26.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results preview. AVC’s 3Q26 revenue of 
TWD59,089m (+20.3% QoQ and +51.8% YoY), a record-high level, 
accounted for 102.3% and 103.0%, respectively, of our previous 
(TWD57,771m, +17.6% QoQ and +48.4% YoY) and the Bloomberg 
consensus forecasts for 3Q26 revenue. We forecast its 3Q26 gross margin 
to be 33.5% (vs. 32.6% in 2Q26 and 26.1% in 3Q25), slightly higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus forecast of 33.3%. We forecast its operating 
profit of TWD16.8bn (+25.0% QoQ and +108.3% YoY), 4.5% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus forecast of TWD16.1bn and we forecast its 
operating margin of 28.5% (vs. 27.4% in 2Q26 and 20.8% in 3Q25). 
Overall, we forecast its net profit of TWD11.6bn (+21.6% QoQ, +117.8% 
YoY), 4.3% higher than the consensus estimate of TWD11.2bn. For 4Q26, 
we forecast revenue to grow 15.1% QoQ (monthly revenue to rise MoM), 
and 1Q27 revenue to grow 2.9% QoQ considering ASIC and GPU projects.

Solid capacity expansion plan in 2027; worsening supply-demand 
environment to bring in a more favourable price trend to the sector. In 
light of the manifold content value from trays, we now expect content value 
by one of its ASICs clients to increase by another 20%. AVC expects the 
revenue drivers in 2027 to include double-sided cold plates, rising liquid 
cooling penetration from ASICs, and more content value wins (one-stop 
shopping model), for example, from CDU, CPU and NPO. As such, AVC 
plans to ramp up its cold plate capacity by 60% by 3Q27 with more 
automated product lines (positive to its further gross margin YoY 
expansion) to support its Vietnam plants, based on current order visibility 
and high utilisation rate. We keep our 2026-28E EPS unchanged.

What we recommend: We reiterate our Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP 
to TWD4,815 (from TWD4,465), still based on a target PER of 30x vs. a 
past-3-year PER range of 5-25x [average PER of 15x]) on its solid earnings 
visibility (earnings CAGR of 45.3% over 2026-2028E; 0.66x PEG) and 
applied to our 1-year forward EPS. Downside risks: slower-than-expected 
progress in high-gross-margin orders, slow AI server adoption, and 
competition.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 8% above consensus, likely as we are 
relatively more aggressive in our revenue, gross and operating margins.

Company profile 
AVC is a global leading comprehensive thermal solutions provider in segments related to desktops, 
laptops, communication, servers, energy and autos. Its main product line includes thermal products 
(thermal modules and fans), chassis, system assembly and peripheral products and hinges. The 
company has been listed on Taiwan’s main board since September 2002.

### Page 5

Asia Vital Components (3017 TT): 2 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 6 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and Chairperson of AVC are the same individual, but we see no issue with its board
quality. Besides, we see the Chairperson has 24 years of experience in this industry, which is a
positive sign from an executive perspective.
Capital management 2 AVC's dividend payout ratio was 40-52% over 2020-25, likely due to its capacity expansionplans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions was 1-3% of total revenue during 2021-25,and we found no suspicious activity.
E Supply chainmanagement 1
AVC has introduced Hazardous Substance Process Management (HSPM) and received
QC080000 certification. In addition, AVC set up a Green Product team to promote HSPM and
formulate environmental regulation to make sure its material sourcing and efficiency are well-
performed.
E Data security 1
To prevent the interruption of information systems and leakage of sensitive information causing
losses to the company and customers, AVC has introduced an information security management
system and passed the ISO 27001 information security management system certification in
2019.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Idea
2027 price hike should be case by case amid push back from some customers:
Management said 2027 pricing negotiations could be mixed across customers even
though capacity is nearly fully loaded. Some need more capacity in 4Q26 and are
willing to pay the new post-hike wafer price. However, the company still faces push
back from some customers, likely tied to their inventories. Our recent checks
suggest  Tier 1 foundry L TA wafer pricing into 2027 could be less than 10%, below

the buyside bogey of a 25-30% blended hike. However, for some tier 2 customers,
the price hike could indeed be around 25-30%. We believe the average like-to-like
L TA wafer price could only increase by  15-20% in 2027. Stay EW.
SOI demand exceeds expectation: The company said 12-inch SOI demand already
exceeds its current expansion plan. The priority is to debottleneck and ramp existing
Missouri capacity. More than half of the newly installed tools have qualified for

volume production, and RF SOI and silicon-photonics products have entered volume
production.
4Q26 could be relatively muted: We expect 4Q26 to be relatively muted since
wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is fully loaded, and
new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin.
With this report, Lucas Wang assumes coverage of GlobalWafers.

Still Better Supply/Demand Dynamics into 2027-28e
3Q26 earnings and 4Q26  outlook review: 3Q26 results missed both our and Visible
Alpha (VA) consensus estimates given the July fire at the Novara, Italy facility, which
disrupted 8-inch wafer production and related SOI output. We expect 4Q26 to be
relatively muted since wafer pricing remains tied to older agreements, existing capacity is
fully loaded and new capacity depreciation would continue to weigh on margin. We had

expected 4Q26 gross margin to be around 25-26%, but we have now reduced that to 22-
23%.

Micron/GWC deal marks a new milestone for GWC
Micron Technology and GlobalWafers announced a deeper strategic partnership on July 9,

2026, centered on a 10-year supply agreement and US$500mn of strategic financing from
Micron to support GlobalWafers' new 12-inch silicon wafer facility in Sherman, Texas. On
its latest earnings call, GWC board proposed executing that financing as up to US$500
million of 10-year privately placed convertible bonds, with Micron as the sole subscriber,
carrying a 0.5% coupon. The agreement secures significant raw silicon wafer capacity for
Micron's long-term manufacturing plans, with the financing linked to the buildout of

GlobalWafers America's (GWA) Sherman facility.
GlobalWafers Chairwoman Doris Hsu described the agreement as the largest and longest
contract in the company's history. The two companies will also collaborate on next-
generation wafer technologies and process innovation. The Sherman plant is notable as
the only US-based source of advanced 12-inch wafers.
The agreement forms part of Micron's broader US$3bn commitment to strengthen the US
semiconductor supply chain and accompanies its target of manufacturing 40% of its

DRAM output domestically by the mid-2030s. We believe GWC is well positioned to

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
support ongoing foundry and memory capacity expansion in the US, not only for Micron
but also for TSMC and Samsung, which are already GWC's two largest customers. We
currently assume Micron/GWC L TA pricing could be US$70-80 on average for the polished
wafer (10-20% above current L TA pricing after discounts), reflecting the higher US cost
base. This would be broadly in line with, or slightly above, L TAs signed in 2021-22.
Current inventory levels remain higher than during the 2021-22 upcycle

when existing L TAs were signed
Notably, customer inventories of 12-inch wafers remain well above levels seen during the
previous industry upcycle. According to SUMCO's inventory trend data (see Exhibit 3 ),
inventory volumes and turnover days were near the lowest levels of the 2014-26 period
during 2021-22, reflecting a lean and highly utilized supply chain. By contrast, the current
cycle presents the opposite picture: shipment volumes are approaching record highs, but

customer inventories are also near record levels, with inventory days and absolute
inventory exceeding those seen even at the peak of the 2021-22 upcycle.
This backdrop differs meaningfully from the 2021-22 environment and, in our view,
supports a more moderate and delayed pricing recovery in 2027 rather than a repeat of
the broad-based increases seen during the prior cycle. In 2021-22, wafer makers held
significant pricing leverage as customer inventories sat near cycle lows despite record

shipment volumes. Fabs consumed nearly all purchased wafers with minimal inventory
buffers, creating a tight supply environment that enabled wafer manufacturers to
implement meaningful price increases across 12-inch wafers.
While shipment volumes currently remain elevated, customers also hold substantial
inventory. This provides a buffer against demand growth and moderate supply disruptions,
reducing the urgency to secure incremental wafer supply. Historically, this dynamic

weakens suppliers' pricing power. Until inventories normalize meaningfully, wafer makers
are likely to have less pricing leverage than they did entering the 2021-22 upcycle. Our
view is that 2027 L TA negotiations will focus primarily on removing current discounts
rather than driving nominal price increases. We broadly expect 2027 industry L TA pricing
increases of 15-20% in average, with Tier 2 customers' wafer price to be raised 25-30% but
Tier 1 customers would only see 5-15%.

Estimate Revisions
We cut our EPS estimates by 14%, 15% and 14% for 2026-28, respectively. The
reductions in our  forecasts primarily reflect lower wafer price hike to Tier 1 customers and
the margin pressure from overseas capacity ramp. However, we also lower our tax rate
assumption from 25% to 20% to reflect the subsidy from the US government.

Valuation Methodology
We lower our price target from NT$900 to NT$770: The price target change mostly
reflects our earnings forecast change over  2026-28. All of of key assumptions in our DCF
model are unchanged, including  a cost of equity of 11% (beta of 1.5, risk premium of 6%
and risk-free rate of 2%), an intermediate growth rate of 13%  and a terminal growth rate
of 4%.
Our bull- and bear-case values are also lowered, from  NT$1,140.00 to NT$975.00 and NT

$450.00 to NT$385.00, respectively, implying P/Es of 32x and 13x 2027e.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

예상 대비 큰 PT/지역 믹스 악화 영향

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 210,000원

현재 주가(10/30) 95,600원

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아 쉬 웠 습 니 다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추 정 치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 11 월 2 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340원에서 1,315원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026년 예상 FCF의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

작성자(한동희)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

### Page 1

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
78 
삼성전자  (005930) 
2026.11.04

STRESS TEST 
[반도체/전기전자] 고의영 
2122-9179 ey.ko@imfnsec.com

목표주가 37만원, ‘28년 기준으로는 56만원까지 열림 
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 37만원으로 커버리지를 개시한다. ‘27 년 BPS에 목표 
P/B 2.3 배를 적용했다. 이는 ‘00년 이후 25년간 평균 P/B의 +2표준편차에 
해당한다[표1]. ‘26~’28년 ROE는 각각 56%, 5 9%, 4 8%로 지난 25년 평균인 
22%를 크게 상회할 전망이며, 고원형 ROE가 장기간 유지될 것으로 판단한다.  
이익 지속성에 대한 믿음이 공고해질 경우 시장의 시야는 ‘28년으로 향할 것이다. 
이 경우 ‘28년 BPS 기준 목표주가는 56만원까지 열릴 수 있다[표2]. 
동사 주가는 ‘26년 6월 고점 대비 10월 저점 기준 43% 하락해 과거 다운사이클 
평균 하락폭인 -41%를 넘어섰다. 특히 과거 8~13개월에 걸쳐 진행됐던 조정이 
이번에는 4개월 만에 나타났다. 시장이 ‘28년 이익 급감을 상정하고 있는 것이다. 
이러한 우려가 지속되는 한, 이익의 눈높이가 높아질수록 멀티플은 저렴해질 
뿐이며, DRAM 가격이 예상보다 더 올라갈 것이라는 소식이 주가의 촉매로 
작용하지 못한다. 결국 주가 반등을 위해서는 시장이 상정하고 있는 다운사이클이 
우려만큼 깊지 않을 것이라는 믿음이 필요한 것으로 본다.

STRESS TEST와 HBM 재평가 
① 당사는 고객들이 어느 정도 가격대에서 LTA를 해지하려는 유인이 생길지 
추정해봤다. Micron의 코멘트를 차용하여 ‘22~’23년 수준의 스트레스 테스트를 
시나리오 분석한 결과, 4Q27 고점 대비 DRAM 가격이 60% 수준 빠져야 해지가 
검토될 수 있다[그림2]. 그마저도 다음 공급 부족기의 물량 배정 우선순위 상실 
등 정성적 요인까지 감안하면 실제 해지 가능성은 더욱 낮은 것으로 판단한다. 
② HBM 비중이 높아질수록 동사의 이익 변동성은 낮아질 것이다. 특히 
중장기적으로 확대될 cHBM은 고객별 수주생산 제품으로 현물시장과 유통 
재고가 없고, 생산에 앞서 물량과 가격이 결정된다. 수요가 둔화되더라도 범용 
메모리처럼 재고 누적과 가격 급락으로 이어지기보다는 발주 조정이나 계약 
재협상을 통해 영향을 받는 구조다.  
동사 HBM 매출은 ‘26년 28조원에서 ‘28년 215조원으로 증가할 전망이다. 
‘27년 HBM 가격 방향성의 윤곽이 잡힌 현 시점에서, 동사 HBM 매출의 변수는 
가격이 아니라 물량이며, 물량은 고객과 사전에 협의되는 구조다. 과거 메모리 
업황에서는 2년 뒤의 이익을 가늠하기 어려웠으나, 이제는 HBM을 통해 중장기 
실적을 논의할 수 있게 됐다는 점, 그 자체에 주목한다. 
만약 동사의 ‘26~’28년 HBM 영업이익에 시기별 TSMC P/E (21배, 15배, 12배)를 
적용하면 HBM 가치는 동사 시가총액의 19%, 69%, 108%를 설명한다[표3]. 
특히 cHBM이 본격화할 ‘28년에는 HBM만으로 현재 시가총액이 설명될 수 
있다는 의미다. 시장이 통상 받아들이는 10배를 타겟팅해도 결과는 크게 다르지 
않다.

반도체(Overweight): Stress Test  
79

‘27년 주가 경로: 회복, 박스권, 재평가 
연말까지는 급락 이후 저점을 높여가는 회복 구간일 전망이다.  
‘27년 상반기까지는 ‘28년 업황 지속성에 대한 우려가 상존할 전망이다. 이 불확실성이 
멀티플을 누르는 동안 ‘27년 이익은 계속 상향되므로 EPS 상승과 멀티플 하락이 상쇄되는 
구간이 이어질 전망이다. 기회비용은 결국 환원수익률로 보상받아야 하므로, ‘27년 1월 신규 
주주환원 정책이 중요한 변곡점이 될 것으로 판단한다.  
당사는 동사가 ‘27~’29년 FCF의 50%를 주주에게 환원하고, 이 중 70%를 배당, 30%를 
자사주 매입/소각으로 쓰는 것으로 가정하여, 현재 주가 기준 ‘27년 15% 수준의 
주주환원수익률을 기대한다. 미국 10년물 국채 금리가 5% 안팎인 환경에서 무위험 금리의 
3배에 달하는 환원수익률, 그중 배당만으로 10% 수준의 수익률은 그 자체로 희소한 투자 
포인트로 볼 수 있다. 
‘27년 하반기 이후에는, 실적 추정의 기준 연도가 ‘28년으로 넘어가고, 이후 신규 팹 물량이 
소량 출하되면서 센티먼트상 우려는 커질 것이다. 그러나 LTA가 이익을 지킨다는 사실이 
숫자로 확인되고 cHBM의 가치가 재평가된다면 순환 할인이 축소될 것으로 판단한다.

업사이드 요인 
① AI 투자 지속성 우려로 최근 주가 하락이 가팔랐던 만큼, 이 부분에 대한 우려가 
완화되면 반등의 속도와 폭이 예상보다 빠를 수 있다 
② 향후 주주환원 규모가 예상보다 더 적극적일 경우 추가적인 업사이드가 될 수 있다. 
혹은 주주환원 이후에도 남는 대규모 FCF의 활용 방안도 촉매가 될 수 있다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

Document 267: AI交易松动，中国资产迎来机会！摩根大通也看好A股：还有10%-15%反弹空间

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易松动，中国资产迎来机会！摩根大通也看好A股：还有10%-15%反弹空间.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

美国中期选举落地、全球 AI 交易松动之际，中国资产迎来新的配置窗⼝。摩根⼤通认为，
估值与仓位重置、政策托底及资⾦轮动将⽀撑 A 股进⼀步反弹，预计仍有 10%-15% 的上涨
空间，维持看好 A 股优于港股，重点关注境内科技股与券商。
摘要：
中国股市在美国中期选举落地后强势反弹，摩根⼤通认为，随着美国中期选举不确定性消散、
全球 AI 交易出现松动，中国资产正在迎来新的配置窗⼝。
11 ⽉ 5 ⽇， A 股三⼤指数震荡⾛⾼，创业板指盘中涨超 3% ，科创 50 ⼀度涨超 5% ，⾄午盘收盘均
有回落。光伏设备、贵⾦属、锂电池等板块领涨。此前的 4 ⽇， A 股亦⼤幅⾛强，沪深 300 上涨
2.3% ，创业板指涨 6.4% ，科创 50 劲升 9.9% ， A 股成交额时隔 25 个交易⽇重返 2 万亿元⼈⺠币。
据追⻛交易台消息，摩根⼤通策略师 Chloe Liu 、 Tim Huang 等⼈在最新报告中将此次反弹归因
于三⼤驱动因素：美国中期选举⻛险落地、估值与仓位⼤幅重置、以及国家队和监管机构⼊市

提振情绪。分析师指出，A 股估值距离 "924" 低点仅回升 8%-10% ，仍处于低位，预计有进⼀步
10%-15% 的反弹空间，并维持对 A 股优于港股的判断，重申看好境内科技股和券商板块。
⾼盛亚洲团队则在题为《中国：逢低买⼊？》的报告中写道，中国国内 AI 相关标的从夏季⾼点回
调近 54% 后， " 看起来越来越具备反弹条件 " ，并看好其 " 中国国内 AI 篮⼦ " （ GSXACADO ）。
中期选举落地，全球 AI 交易格局⽣变
美国中期选举结果出炉，市场不确定性随之消退。摩根⼤通认为，受⽴法约束与总统否决权限
制，此次选举结果不会对美国整体政策格局带来实质性改变，市场此前已对⺠主党横扫两院的
情形有所定价。历史数据显⽰，中国股票在美国中期选举结束后往往倾向于反弹，原因在于不
确定性的消散。
更值得关注的是，全球 AI 交易正出现松动迹象。德克萨斯州占美国 2030 年前规划数据中⼼容量
逾 25% ，⽽该州新届政府已释放出对新建数据中⼼采取更严格限制⽴场的信号。摩根⼤通指

出，随着全球 AI 交易争议升温，中国资产有望从中受益 —— 当前局⾯或引发空头回补及资⾦轮
动。
报告同时强调，中国股票本⾝可提供与全球 AI 交易的多元化配置价值，⽽中国 AI 的本⼟化发展有
其独⽴轨迹，对美国 AI 资本开⽀的依赖程度相对有限。
估值与仓位双重重置，反弹空间尚存
摩根⼤通认为，估值和仓位层⾯的全⾯重置，是⽀撑进⼀步反弹的重要结构性基础。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

估 值⽅ ⾯ ， 即 便 经 历 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇ 的 上 涨 ， MSCI 中 国 和 沪 深 300 指数 仍 仅 较 "924" 低 点 ⾼ 出
8%-10% ，估值修复空间尚存。⾼盛⽅⾯数据则显⽰，其中国国内 AI 篮⼦的远期市盈率已从 7 ⽉
峰值时约 53 倍压缩⾄当前约 25 倍，回落⾄ 2025 年夏季 AI ⾏情启动前的⽔平，篮⼦指数较 7 ⽉初
⾼点累计下跌约 55% ，年内涨幅⼏近悉数回吐。
仓位⽅⾯，摩根⼤通列出三组数据：融资余额从 6 ⽉的 3 万亿元⼈⺠币回落⾄当前约 2.3 万亿元，
已退回⾄ 2025 年 9 ⽉⽔平，今年以来的杠杆积累基本消化；北向资⾦持有的 A 股 IT 板块敞⼝从 6 ⽉
末的 35% 降⾄ 9 ⽉末的 27% ；境内公募基⾦前⼗⼤重仓中， IT 持仓占⽐从 6 ⽉约 60% 降⾄ 9 ⽉
48% ，摩根⼤通估计 10 ⽉进⼀步降⾄约 40% 。这⼀仓位重置意味着较⼤规模的场外观望资⾦具
备⼊市潜⼒，有望推动科技板块的阶段性反弹。
⾼盛同样将仓位视为重要的结构性⽀撑。该⾏报告显⽰，全球主动基⾦对中国的配置持续下滑

⾄ 5.5% ，接近过去五年低点，处于 2015 年以来第 6 百分位，较 2020 年约 15% 的峰值配置缩⽔约
三分之⼆，相对基准低配 215 个基点。⾼盛分析师 Finn Dattenberg-Doyle 和 Janvi Sharma 在报
告中写道：
政策底部逐步夯实， " 国家队 " 是关键变量
在市场内⽣动⼒之外，政策层⾯的⽀撑同样不可忽视。科创 50 指数较 6 ⽉⾼点已累计回调约
50% ，并于 8 ⽉与美股 AI 反弹⾏情脱钩。进⼊ 10 ⽉后，国家队加速买⼊ ETF ，单⽉净买⼊规模达
3300 亿元⼈⺠币。下半年以来的 ETF 净流⼊规模，已相当于上半年净流出的 35% ，仍有约 9000
亿元⼈⺠币的潜在 " ⼲⽕药 " 。
本周⼈⺠银⾏与证监会释放的信号，进⼀步表明监管层有意为市场下⾏托底。摩根⼤通将此定
性为 " 不是⽕箭筒，但是更坚实的地板 " 。
市场营销反馈：关注点从 AI 向多元化板块扩散
摩根⼤通在⾹港和新加坡路演过程中收集到的投资者反馈显⽰，⼤多数投资者认同在政策托底

后下⾏空间有限，离岸投资者的增配意愿相对更强。
投资者的关注点正从 AI 向更多元化的板块扩散。银⾏股受到最多关注，其次是医疗健康，以及部
分提供可观股东回报和较⾼盈利能⻅度的消费类公司。
在个股层⾯，摩根⼤通重申旗下中国市场⼗⼤优选标的，同时新增⼀份 AI 相关股票筛选名单 ——
筛选条件为：摩根⼤通给予 " 增持 " 评级、 2027 年预期市盈率低于 20 倍、 2027 年预期每股盈利增
速超过 30% ，且股价较 52 周⾼点跌幅超 40% 。⾼盛则重点推介 GSXACADO 篮⼦，成分股覆盖拓
荆科技、⻓川科技、华虹半导体、中微公司、⻓鑫存储和寒武纪等。
" 从仓位⻆度来看，经历近期调整后，布局条件看起来更具建设性。 "

Document 295: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Economy 
Kicking and screaming: Child care costs persist 
20 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Child care remains a growing financial burden for many households, even as growth in the median payment has moderated, 
according to Bank of America payments data. More families are paying more than $2,000 per month for care, suggesting 
affordability pressures are becoming increasingly concentrated among households facing the highest expenses. 
• Child care cost pressures vary significantly across the country. Nashville and Jacksonville, FL posted the fastest year-over-year 
growth among major metro areas, while New York and Washington DC saw the biggest decrease in median payment growth, 
according to Bank of America payments data. 
• Limited supply may continue to constrain access to child care for some households. Child care employment fell 0.7% year-over-
year in September, suggesting provider capacity remains under pressure and could continue to affect household finances and 
labor force participation.

Child care costs outpace rent and medical services 
Child care remains a significant expense for many families. In September, the growth in child care and other school-related fees 
surpassed other major household expenses such as rent and medical services, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) 
(Exhibit 1). 
Yet, while child care prices continue to rise in official inflation data, median household payments have largely moderated since 
February, according to Bank of America data (Exhibit 2). More importantly, the growth in the number of households paying for 
child care has fallen since the start of 2025. 
Exhibit 1: The cost of child care has surpassed other services such as 
rent in the consumer price index

The decline in the number of households paying for child care may also reflect families seeking alternatives to formal care 
arrangements. Some may be relying more heavily on relatives, shifting parents’ work schedules or dropping out of the workforce 
altogether to care for their children in response to elevated costs. This could explain why, among all income groups paying for 
care, the number of lower-income households has seen the biggest decline, falling 2.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September 
(Exhibit 3).

More families face larger monthly bills 
Yet even as fewer households pay for child care, the share of those shelling out more than $2,000 per month has increased 
substantially (Exhibit 4). In fact, it has more than doubled since 2023, reaching 7.7% in 2026 year-to-date. Meanwhile, the share 
of those paying $1,000 or less has declined, suggesting higher monthly payments are becoming more common even as the 
national median grows more slowly. 
Exhibit 4: The share of households paying >$2000 a month for child care has more than doubled since 2023 
Monthly child care payments by amount (annual average, share of households, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Rising costs take a bigger bite out of households’ paychecks 
What makes child care “affordable”? According to the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, child care is considered 
affordable if it accounts for no more than 7% of family income. Yet, in Bank of America data, the average child care cost was 8% 
in September.  
While an 8% share may not appear dramatically above the federal affordability benchmark of 7%, it represents an average across 
households’ income levels. For families in high-cost metro areas or those paying more than $2,000 per month, the burden is 
likely considerably higher.

Notably, lower- and middle-income households see outsized shares of their pay dedicated to child care in Bank of America 
payments data, though this has moderated slightly from 2025 (Exhibit 5).  
Exhibit 5: In September, the average child care payment was 8.6% of lower- and middle-income total households’ pay, whereas for higher-income 
families it accounted for 7.3% 
Average child care payment as a share of pay in September by income (annual, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Where child care costs are climbing 
Child care pressures also differ substantially by location. In Bank of America payments data, Nashville posted the fastest YoY 
median payment growth in September, followed by Jacksonville, FL and Phoenix (Exhibit 6). This dispersion of growth across the 
country suggests child care cost pressures are no longer confined to historically expensive coastal markets.  
Interestingly, New York and Washington DC saw the sharpest decline in growth this year. Why might this be? The supply side of 
the market may help explain why – for example, New York City recently introduced universal Pre-K.1  
Exhibit 6: Nashville and Jacksonville, FL saw the greatest increase in median child care payment growth among select cities in September 
Median child care payments by core-based statistical area in September (YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

  I NSTI TUTE

Fewer providers, higher costs 
As of September 2026, employment in child care services was down 0.7% from a year earlier, suggesting the sector’s workforce 
recovery has stalled. Fewer workers can constrain the number of available places or the hours providers offer. That may leave 
families facing longer searches, fewer workable arrangements or continued price pressure. In turn, access to care can influence 
parents’ ability to enter, remain in or expand their participation in the workforce. 
Exhibit 7: Employment within child care services fell 0.7% YoY in September 
Employees by sector (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
What are the economic implications of rising child care costs? 
Child care costs may have important labor market implications for families with young children. Of those households paying for 
child care in 2026, more than half of them were Older Millennials (52%), followed by Younger Millennials (20%) and Gen X (16%), 
according to Bank of America payments data. This means child care costs weigh more heavily on Older Millennials. 
The share of Older Millennial households receiving more than one paycheck has continued to grow from a year earlier, but the 
pace of that improvement has moderated over the past three months (Exhibit 8). Given that this group accounts for the majority 
of households paying for child care in Bank of America payments data, they may offer early signs of how affordability affects 
their employment decisions. 
Exhibit 8: The share of Older Millennial households paying for child care that have more than one person with a paycheck has declined since 2021  
Share of households with a child care payment with more than one person with a paycheck (%, monthly, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Child care challenges can weigh on women’s employment 
According to data from the American Time Use Survey, women continue to perform significantly more child care and household 
work than men, even as their participation in paid work has increased. This suggests that persistent gaps in access and 
affordability may have disproportionate consequences for women’s employment. Indeed, the percentage of women who cited 
child care as a reason for temporary unemployment was nearly three times higher than men’s in 2026, though the average fell 
from 2025, according to data from the BLS. 
Yet labor force participation data indicates a more nuanced picture. Despite persistent child care pressures, mothers are 
participating in the workforce at rates that remain above their 2019 averages.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash and checks.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Contributors 
Taylor Bowley 
Economist, Bank of America Institute

TRPR_82201_20261022

Document 412: RBNZの予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZドル相場は一段と弱含むか ～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照_7d032c38

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBNZの予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZドル相場は一段と弱含むか ～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照_7d032c38.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 28 日

1/4 
RBNZ の予想外の利上げ見送りで、NZ ドル相場は一段と弱含むか 
～追加利上げの時期や総選挙を巡る不透明感が重なり、豪ドルとの対照的な動きが続くか～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政
策金利を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。RBNZ は７月に約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、
９月にも追加利上げを実施するなど金融引き締めを強めてきた。このため、今回の据え置き
は３会合ぶりであり、金融市場の事前予想に反する決定となった。 
○ ７-９月のインフレ率は前年比＋3.7％と前期から鈍化したものの、コアインフレ率は加速す
るなど、インフレ圧力は依然として根強いことが確認された。このため、金融市場において
は RBNZ が今回利上げに動くことを織り込む動きが広がりをみせていた。こうしたなか、 今回
の決定は４対３の僅差となったほか、声明文や総裁会見でも追加利上げの必要性が示された
ものの、そのタイミングについては不透明感が残る。 
○ また、11 月７日の総選挙を控え、RBNZ の政策目標の見直しが争点となっていることも、 先行
きの政策運営を巡る不透明感につながっている。金融市場では、予想外の金利据え置きに対
する 「失望」 に加え、 金融政策が後手に回るとの警戒感もＮＺドル相場の重しとなっている。
当面はＮＺドル相場の上値が重い展開が続くと見込まれ、オセアニア通貨のなかでも豪ドル
とＮＺドルが対照的な動きをみせる可能性がある。

【RBNZは事前予想に反して、10 月の定例会合で３会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置く決定】 
ニュージーランド準備銀行（RBNZ）は、10 月 27～28 日に開催した定例理事会において、政策金
利であるオフィシャル・キャッシュ・レート（ＯＣＲ）を2.75％に据え置くことを決定した。ニュ
ージーランドでは、2025 年以降にインフレ率が加速し、RBNZ の定める目標レンジ（１～３％）の
上限を上回る伸びで推移している。 さらに、 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油価格の上昇がエネルギ
ーインフレを招いたことから、RBNZ は７月の定例会合で約３年ぶりの利上げに舵を切り、 ９月会合
でも追加利上げに動くなど、金融引き締めを強化してきた（注１）。このため、RBNZ による金利据

注１ ９月２日付レポート「RBNZ は緩やかな引き締め示唆､NZ ドル相場の行方はどうなる？」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654752.html）

2/4 
え置きは３会合ぶりとなる。 
RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、 ９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、 追加利上げに含みを持たせる考
えを示していた。一方で、予め決められた方針に固執せず、追加利上げのタイミングは極めて不確
実と述べるとともに、これまでの利上げの効果などを見極める考えをみせた。声明文でも、２会合
連続の利上げ実施を受けて、 先行きは大幅利上げに動く必要性は低下したとの認識を示すなど、「タ
カ派」姿勢が後退している様子がうかがえた。こうした姿勢の変化もあり、ＲＢＮＺによる断続的
な利上げ実施にもかかわらず、その後のＮＺドル相場は調整の動きを強めた。 
同国では、11 月７日に議会（代議院：一院制）の総選挙が実施されるが、RBNZ のマンデート（政
策目標）が争点のひとつとなっていることも、ＮＺドル相場が調整の動きを強める一因となった。
最大野党の左派・労働党を中心とする政
党連合は、マンデートに持続可能な雇用
の最大化を再び加えることを公約に掲げ
ている。世論調査では、右派の国民党を
中心とする与党連合、左派連合の競り合
いが続いており、新興政党・オポチュニ
ティ党の動向が注目されている（注２）。
足元の雇用環境をみると、 直近４-６月の
失業率が 10 年ぶりの高水準となってお
り（図１）、 仮にマンデートが変更されれ
ば、利上げのハードルが高まる可能性が
ある。こうした事情もＮＺドル相場の重しになったと考えられる。 
一方で、RBNZ のブレマン総裁は、９月の定例会合後の記者会見において、選挙結果が政策運営に
影響を与えることはないとの見方を示し
た。このため、金融市場では今回の定例
会合を占ううえで、物価動向に注目した。

3/4 
伸びとなるなど、 インフレ圧力が高まっている様子が確認された。このため、 金融市場ではRBNZ が
